## Assistant

I'll keep going from here. The Linux baseline is in place, and Android and FreeBSD stay for a later pass.

`--localize --to` translates sentences only. Code, paths, flags, URLs, and hashes are masked and put back unchanged. Launcher logs use the same rule: full sentences can be Japanese, and `serve.sh: listening on` stays English so the readiness check can match it.

**Android and FreeBSD, after Linux.** Linux stays the fully supported platform. Android/Termux already has an arm64 CPU asset with subprocess support and `RUNPATH $ORIGIN`, a wake lock, and host tools, because a container is unlikely on a phone. That asset has not been run on a phone. A real pass still needs router child processes under Android's process limits, one tool call, the UI, a 16 KB page-size check, and a decision about Vulkan. FreeBSD would be detected as `freebsd-x64` or `freebsd-arm64`, then miss a release asset and fall through the Linux build and `/proc` memory checks. A first port would build it as FreeBSD, read RAM and CPU counts with `sysctl`, and leave tools on the host. No release hash exists to invent.

**Size.** A file that does not fit can be a smaller quant. AgentHorse is the optional pick `agenthorse` at Q3_K_S (2.07 GB). Q4_K_M is 2.71 GB and was left unpinned. [Meanblock/JEV-CPU](https://huggingface.co/Meanblock/JEV-CPU) ships code, not weights. Its accurate brain is a 4B model, so the optional pick `jev` is Qwen3-0.6B Q8_0 (639 MB) in `models-optional/jev/`. `serve.sh` does not start the JEV scorer and does not replace Laya. Neither file has been run here.

Also in this pass: the project shell scripts are `/bin/sh` (`dash -n` and `sh tests/test_cpu.sh` passed), `install.sh` / `install.ps1` refuse a missing or mismatched digest, the config form is `python3 scripts/panel.py`, and `agent.py` reads `.cache/tools.json` so an empty `--cwd` still sends `x-tool-cwd`. The public installer stays closed until `EXPECTED_SHA256` is filled from a published archive.

Checked here: digest rejection, the panel over HTTP, and a Podman probe where `exec -w /work` from `/` can read the mount. A plain `exec` from the host directory also landed in `/work` on this Podman, so the old "current working directory" failure did not show up in that probe. There is no `llama-server` binary in `bin/`, so the full server readiness test was not run.

## User

the background agent can definitely look into possible FreeBSD/Android issues

## Assistant

A first pass already sketched Android and FreeBSD. I'll send a closer read of the scripts we just changed, looking for concrete breakage on those two platforms.

A closer audit of the Android and FreeBSD paths is running against the scripts as they are now. It will not change the tree.

The first pass already found the blockers:

- **Android/Termux** already has an arm64 CPU asset with subprocess support and `RUNPATH $ORIGIN`, plus a wake lock and host tools. It has never been run on a phone. Still open: router child processes under Android's process limits, one real tool call, the UI, a 16 KB page-size check, and whether Vulkan is worth a separate build.
- **FreeBSD** would be detected as `freebsd-x64` or `freebsd-arm64`, then miss a release asset and fall through `scripts/build-linux.sh` and the `/proc` memory checks. A later port would build it as FreeBSD, read RAM and CPU counts with `sysctl`, and leave tools on the host. There is no release hash to copy.

Linux stays the fully supported platform until that audit is in.

  printf '%s\n' '#!/bin/sh' 'echo start' > /tmp/cbl-src/code-bootstraps-llama.cpp-main/start.sh
  tar -C /tmp/cbl-src -czf /tmp/cbl-inst.tgz code-bootstraps-llama.cpp-main
fi
sum=$(sha256sum /tmp/cbl-inst.tgz | cut -d' ' -f1)
rm -rf /tmp/cbl-home /data 2>/dev/null || true
mkdir -p /tmp/cbl-home
HOME=/tmp/cbl-home PREFIX=/data/data/com.termux/files/usr \
  INSTALL_URL=file:///tmp/cbl-inst.tgz INSTALL_SHA256=$sum INSTALL_NO_START=1 \
  sh install.sh
test -f /tmp/cbl-home/code-bootstraps-llama.cpp/start.sh
test ! -e /data/data/com.termux/files/usr/start.sh
echo "ok termux prefix spared"
export GIT_AUTHOR_NAME='Brian Fundakowski Feldman'
export GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL='159288071+brianreborn@users.noreply.github.com'
export GIT_COMMITTER_NAME="$GIT_AUTHOR_NAME"
export GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL="$GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL"
git add install.sh README.md
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
Keep the installer off the Termux prefix

Termux exports PREFIX as its usr directory. install.sh was using that as
the unpack destination. INSTALL_PREFIX, or the home default, is used
instead.
EOF
git status --short
git log -1 --oneline

## Assistant

The Android and FreeBSD audit is in. Linux stays the supported platform, and nothing else from that list was ported. One installer bug from the audit is committed as `03eaf27`.

On Termux, `PREFIX` is the Termux usr directory. `install.sh` was treating that as the unpack destination, so a fetch-and-run could have written over `/data/data/com.termux/files/usr`. It now uses `INSTALL_PREFIX` or `~/code-bootstraps-llama.cpp`. A local check installed into the home directory and left the Termux path alone.

These still block a later port:

- **FreeBSD** is detected as `freebsd-x64` or `freebsd-arm64`, then misses a release asset and falls into `scripts/build-linux.sh`. An arm64 configure fails in llama.cpp. A GPU device can also push that fallback build to CUDA or Vulkan and abort. With no `/proc`, RAM reads as 0 and the auto profile becomes `default`. Base FreeBSD has `fetch` and `sha256`, not `curl` and `sha256sum`.
- **Android** detection is split: `fetch-llama.sh` keys off `PREFIX`, while `serve.sh` also treats `ANDROID_ROOT` as Android. Unset `PREFIX` fetches the glibc Linux binary. `setsid` is not in a stock Termux install, so the server may not be its own session. The wake lock needs `termux-api`. A tree on `/sdcard` is noexec. The on-device `build-termux.sh` path does not pass `GPU=vulkan` through, and it does not use the Android CPU variant list.

Leave these until a phone or a FreeBSD machine is available: router child processes under Android's process limits, 16 KB page alignment of the published asset, the A57 thread count, and Vulkan. The pack script is still a Linux x86_64 maintainer tool because the archive uses GNU tar.

## Assistant

This session turned the release blockers into four local commits on `main`. Nothing was pushed.

**Launchers and tools** (`d53deab`). The project shell scripts are `/bin/sh` now, including the tests. `dash -n` is clean and `sh tests/test_cpu.sh` passed. With Podman or Docker, `serve.sh` starts the server in `/`, checks that `/work` is usable, and stops if that check fails instead of falling back to the host. It records the mount in `.cache/tools.json`. `agent.py` sends `x-tool-cwd` from that file when `--cwd` is empty. On Linux, `VARIANT=auto` fetches a CUDA or Vulkan build when a GPU device is present. Launcher sentences can be Japanese. Paths, flags, hashes, tool names, and the line `serve.sh: listening on` stay as written. `--localize --to` follows the same rule.

**Installers** (`48925f6`, `03eaf27`). `install.sh` and `install.ps1` check a sha256 before unpacking and update an existing copy only when the digest changes. `EXPECTED_SHA256` is still empty, so the public one-liner refuses to download until a published archive is hashed. On Termux, the installer no longer uses `PREFIX` as the destination, because that variable is the Termux usr tree.

**Config and models** (`bcbccdc`). `python3 scripts/panel.py` is a local form that writes `.cache/panel.env`. An environment variable set in the shell still wins. **Checked here.** Digest rejection, including a truncated archive. The panel, over HTTP. A Podman probe where `exec -w /work` from `/` can read the mount. There is no `llama-server` binary in `bin/`, so the full readiness test and a real agent tool call were not run. **Left for later.** Android and FreeBSD were audited and not ported. FreeBSD has no release asset and would fall through the Linux build and `/proc` memory checks. The Android asset still needs a phone: router child processes, one tool call, the UI, and a 16 KB page-size check. Agent-Reach was not added. The llama.cpp submodule was not switched.

## Assistant

No. Parking the old coder file does not unload the other models. `35270d5` pins AgentHorse at Q4_K_M (2.71 GB). The boxes below are weight files only. The KV cache sits on top of them.

`--pick agenthorse` only moves files. The previous coder leaves `models/coder/` and is not loaded:

```
models/coder/                         models-inactive/coder/
+---------------------------+         +----------------------+
| AgentHorse Q4_K_M  2.71 GB|         | Qwen3.5-2B     1.28 GB|
+---------------------------+         +----------------------+
        served                                parked
```

What stays in RAM depends on the profile. `lowram` is the full unload (`--models-max 1`). `moderate` and `default` pin Laya and still have two rotating slots (`--models-max 3`).

```
lowram, coder in use                 moderate / default, both chat roles used

+---------------------------+        +---------------------------+
| AgentHorse         2.71 GB|        | AgentHorse         2.71 GB|
+---------------------------+        +---------------------------+
| (general unloaded)        |        | LFM2.5 general      0.73 GB|
| (decision unloaded)       |        +---------------------------+
+---------------------------+        | Laya decision       0.45 GB|  stays loaded
                                     +---------------------------+
peak weights: 2.71 GB                peak weights: about 3.9 GB
```

`LANGUAGE_MODE=swap` is a third mechanism. It replaces the general file with the Japanese model. It does not unload the coder or Laya.

```
[general]  Japanese SFT 0.53 GB   takes the general slot
[coder]    AgentHorse     2.71 GB   unchanged
[decision] Laya           0.45 GB   still resident on moderate/default
```

So 2.71 GB is the whole weight peak only on `lowram`. On the other two profiles it is the coder slot, with Laya still resident and the general model able to sit beside it.

## Assistant

One command now brings the stack up, including the memory-lock right.

- Linux and macOS: `./start.sh` (the installer and `start.command` both end in that script).
- Windows: `start.bat`, which runs `scripts\start.ps1`.

On Linux, the first launch in a terminal asks for sudo once and runs `scripts/raise.sh`. On Windows it asks for administrator once and runs `scripts\raise.ps1`. The server starts either way. After a successful grant it writes `.cache/raise.stamp`, so the next launch does not ask again while this login still has the old limit. Sign in again before locking can succeed. `RAISE=1` asks again. `RAISE=0` skips the prompt. Termux and macOS skip it. A declined prompt does not stamp, so the next launch asks again.

`sh tests/test_raise_gate.sh` passed, including a terminal check that the prompt happens once. This is commit `7b2b5bd`, local only.

## Assistant

`HOST=0.0.0.0` and `HOST=::` work. A single external address does not work with the rest of this stack.

`serve.sh` passes `HOST` straight through as llama-server's `--host`. `0.0.0.0` and `::` listen on every interface. The readiness check, `recover.py`, and the page `start.sh` opens all use `127.0.0.1`, and that address still answers because those binds include loopback. Another machine can open `http://<this-machine>:9931`. The router is the only process on that address. The general, coder, and decision processes stay on `127.0.0.1`. The API key travels as plain HTTP, and `serve.sh` warns when `HOST` is not loopback. A host firewall can still drop the packets after the bind succeeds.

`HOST=192.168.x.x` listens only on that address. The health check uses that address, so the server can still become ready. The Web UI URL and the agent's default URL stay `http://127.0.0.1:9931`, and nothing is listening there. `scripts/agent.py` also refuses to send the API key over plain HTTP to any host other than `127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, or `::1`.

llama-server accepts a comma-separated list, so `HOST=127.0.0.1,192.168.1.20` keeps the local page working and also listens on that LAN address. The agent on another machine still will not send the key over plain HTTP. `--host` on the command line is refused; `HOST=` is the setting that is applied.

}

## Assistant

A remote model for rare, one-shot work is a small feature. The costly part is deciding what single prompt to send. The local router should stay the only thing that runs on ordinary turns.

**How it would work.** Leave `REMOTE_URL` and a key file empty by default. Call it only when you ask, for example `python3 scripts/agent.py --remote "…"`, or when you name that step in the task. That call is one non-streaming completion: one assembled prompt, no tools, no transcript. The reply comes back as a single message, and the local coder applies it. A timeout and a size cap stop a hung remote call from stalling the loop. Off loopback the URL has to be `https`. `scripts/agent.py` already refuses to send an API key over plain `http` except to `127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, and `::1`.

**What would make it expensive.** If the remote model also runs tools, or receives the local history on every step, the wire traffic is the same bloat you want to avoid. The packer has to send the task plus the few files that matter. An automatic "this looks hard" classifier is a separate choice and should stay off.

That is about a day of work: the call, the empty-by-default setting, and a fake-server test. I have not started it.

Thinking can already change per request on this llama-server, without a restart. A chat body with `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking` set to `true` turns it on for that request. `reasoning_effort` set to `"none"` turns it off. The preset still loads with `reasoning = off`, and that value is only the default. `scripts/agent.py` never sends the field, and it still waits for the whole JSON body, so thoughts are not shown as they generate. A `REASONING=` default is applied when `serve` writes the preset, so that switch still needs a restart.

The earlier external-`HOST` fix is committed locally as `2bc16f3` and is not pushed. `HOST=192.168.1.20` now listens on `127.0.0.1` as well, so the Web UI and the agent on this machine still connect. `HOST=0.0.0.0` and `HOST=::` are unchanged. `tests/test_bindhost.sh` passed under `dash` and `/bin/sh`. The PowerShell side was not executed here.

- Read: /home/green/.grok/memory-v2/workspaces/code-bootstraps-llama-cpp-0aed0c61/topics/agent-loop.md

## Assistant

Yes. The handoff can be automatic, and it should fire on the loops this agent already counts. It should fire once, into a remote session you have attached, and it should send a short brief rather than the local transcript.

`scripts/agent.py` already notices a stuck coder. The same tool call in the same epoch is skipped, and after two skips the next step is forced to stop calling tools and print a one-line summary. That path currently returns success. The step limit does the same. Those two points are the flounder signal. A third is worth adding: three tool failures in a row with no file written. A fluent but wrong answer is not detectable from here without another model call, so that stays out.

When one of those fires, and a remote login is configured, the agent stops the local loop and sends one request to the attached remote session. The brief is the original task, the paths touched, the last distinct tool result, and the call that got stuck. The remote reply is printed and saved on that session. It is not fed back into another automatic remote call. With no login configured, the agent prints that it is stuck and stops.

The remote context lives in named sessions under named logins. A login is a base URL, a model name, and a key file under `.secrets/remote/`. A session is a conversation on that login. The local file remembers the active login, the active session, and the remote conversation id when the provider has one. When it does not, the session stores only its own briefs and replies, and those are what the next call resends. Local tool chatter never enters that record. Switching login or session is how you keep two powerful models, or two lines of work, from sharing a context.

Six commands, as the first word of an agent run:

- `/remote` shows the active login, session, and whether auto-handoff is armed.
- `/remote login <name>` attaches a credential profile.
- `/remote session <name>` attaches a session. Later escalations continue that conversation.
- `/remote new` starts a fresh session on the current login and leaves the old one in place.
- `/remote on` and `/remote off` arm or disarm the automatic handoff. Off still leaves `/remote take` available.
- `/remote take` sends the current task now. `/remote ask <text>` sends that text and does not run the local coder first.

Auto-handoff stays off until `/remote on`, and it runs at most once per local task. The agent is still a single run, so these are commands at launch. There is no chat prompt yet where you could type them mid-loop. I have not built this.

## User

Beautiful, let's just make sure we're not missing anything major here

## Tools

- Read: /home/green/code-bootstraps-llama.cpp/scripts/agent.py (161-190)
- Search: decision|LANGUAGE_MODE|interpret
- Search: \.secrets|\.cache

## Assistant

The design holds. Five holes would make the handoff wrong if we skip them. The rest can wait.

**The remote model advises. It does not run tools.** Reissuing the request means one completion on the attached session. The reply is printed here. It is not executed, and it is not given this machine's `/tools` endpoint or API key. A powerful model with a shell on this box is a second agent loop and puts the whole transcript back on the wire. Applying the reply stays a later local run, or a person.

**The brief has to include what this run already changed.** The coder often edits files before it gets stuck. The original task alone lets the remote undo that work or repeat it. Send the task, the paths written this run, a short diff of those paths, the last distinct tool result, and the call that stuck. Cap each piece. Label tool output as untrusted data so a file the coder read cannot steer the remote model.

**Do not send secrets.** Skip `.secrets/` and the remote key files. The local API key never goes in the brief. Tool output is the leak: a `read_file` of an env file would otherwise be uploaded. Drop any tool result whose path is under `.secrets/`, and truncate the rest.

**A session belongs to one login and one workspace.** The record is `login / session / this checkout's root`. Attaching `work/compiler` from another directory continues the conversation and says which directory the new brief is from. Two agent processes must not write the same session file at once. Save the brief before the call, so a crash does not send it twice and a failed call can be retried with `/remote take`.

**A stuck local run is not success.** Today the repeat path and the step limit print a summary and return 0. After this change that summary still prints, then the remote is called if `/remote on` and a login exists. The process exits 0 only after the remote reply is saved. No login, remote down, or `/remote off` exits nonzero and leaves the brief on disk for `/remote take`. With `LANGUAGE_MODE=interpret`, send the English task the coder already saw, and pass the remote reply back through the same translation as a local answer.

One protocol is enough: an OpenAI-compatible chat completion, HTTPS off loopback, the same rule `scripts/agent.py` already enforces. A big model on another machine over plain HTTP needs HTTPS in front of it. Stream that one reply so a long think is visible. Cap how many briefs and replies a session resends. The decision model does not judge these turns.

No code was changed.

from pathlib import Path
p = Path("/tmp/cbl-issues")
issues = {
"01-live-remote-test.md": """# title
Live test of /remote against a real client

# body
Kind: test

`tests/test_agent.py` and `tests/test_remote.py` cover the handoff with a fake HTTP server and a fake executable. Nothing here has called a real grok, agy, claude, codex, or paid HTTPS model.

Do not point the remote login at this same `scripts/agent.py`. A stuck local coder would send the brief back into the same loop.

Check, on a machine where that client is already logged in:

- `/remote login work grok` then `/remote ask` starts (`--session-id`) and a second ask resumes (`--resume`).
- The same for `claude` (`--session-id` then `--resume`), `agy` (`-p` then `--conversation`), and `codex` (`codex exec` then `codex exec resume`).
- An image, a wav, and a pdf are named in the brief as `path=` / `kind=` and are not inlined.
- `/remote on` after two identical tool repeats sends one brief and then stops.
""",
"02-scoped-remote-advisor.md": """# title
Remote handoff stays an advisor

# body
Kind: scoped-out

The HTTP login is one chat completion. It is not given this machine's `/tools` endpoint or the local API key. The reply is printed. It is not executed.

`grok`, `agy`, `claude`, and `codex` are launched with their own session flags and no skip-permissions / danger flag. Extra arguments typed on `/remote login` are appended for people who want that. A file's kind does not pick a different binary. `kind=image|audio|video|pdf` is only a label on the path.

`codex -i` is used for images. Audio, video, and pdf stay `path=` lines because that flag is documented for images.
""",
"03-scoped-flounder-and-commands.md": """# title
Flounder detection and /remote commands stay narrow

# body
Kind: scoped-out

A stuck run is two identical skipped tool calls, three tool failures in a row, or the step limit. A fluent wrong answer is not detected. The decision model is not asked to judge the turn.

`/remote` is the launch prompt (`python3 scripts/agent.py "/remote ..."`). There is no mid-run prompt. `/remote take` sends the brief saved from the last stuck run.

An HTTP login off loopback must be `https`. Plain `http` is refused except for 127.0.0.1, localhost, and ::1, same as the local API key.
""",
"04-limit-remote-tokens.md": """# title
Remote context and briefs stay small on purpose

# body
Kind: limitation

An HTTP call resends only the last reply, clipped to 400 characters, plus one brief of at most 1600 characters. The completion cap is 384 tokens. Media files are paths, not bytes. Text snippets in an HTTP brief are clipped.

Local clients keep their own transcript via the CLI session id. If `grok` or `claude` exits non-zero, the id we generated is not stored, so the next call starts again. `agy` and `codex` ids are taken from their output or, for agy, from `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/cache/last_conversations.json` (override with `AGENT_AGY_CACHE`). If that id is missing, the next call starts again.
""",
"05-gguf-home.md": """# title
Shared GGUF store outside the checkout

# body
Kind: needed

Models should live in one directory every checkout can use. Planned default: `$XDG_DATA_HOME/gguf` or `$HOME/.local/share/gguf` (`GGUF_HOME` overrides). Layout: `models/<role>/`, `models-optional/`, `models-inactive/`. `MODELS_DIR` still overrides a role directory.

Also still needed: `scripts/cache-model.sh` and `scripts/cache-model.ps1` to move named GGUF files into that store without overwriting a different file. Do not use a disposable cache directory for the weights. Verification stamps can stay in the checkout's `.cache/verified`.

Tests that assume `ROOT/models` must set `GGUF_HOME` to the sandbox.
""",
"06-configure-console.md": """# title
Console for every major setting

# body
Kind: needed

`scripts/configure.sh` and `scripts/configure.ps1` should ask for the major settings and write only non-default keys into `.cache/panel.env`, which is already `: "${KEY:=value}"` so an explicit environment wins. Enter keeps the default and omits the key.

The web panel `FIELDS` list is still shorter than the launcher settings (profile, models max, variant, tools, gpu layers, port, threads, context, reasoning, host, gguf home, load mode, and so on). `LANGUAGE_MODE=auto` is offered by the panel and is not a serve mode (`native|swap|interpret|off`).
""",
"07-reasoning-switch.md": """# title
Easy reasoning switch, off by default, thoughts as they generate

# body
Kind: needed

`config/models-preset.ini` sets `reasoning = off` on coder, language, and locale. That stays the default.

llama-server already accepts `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking` on one request, and streams `delta.reasoning_content` when reasoning is not off. `scripts/agent.py` does not send that field and does not read the stream. A `REASONING=on` (or `auto`) overlay for general and coder, applied at serve start, plus printing `[think]` tokens on stderr, is the switch. Empty and `off` add nothing, so the current preset stays put. Changing the server default still means restarting the router. The per-request field does not.
""",
"08-specialized-models.md": """# title
Pull an extra specialized model without replacing the defaults

# body
Kind: needed

The running bootstrap should be able to ask the user which extra model to add, then download it only after a sha256 is known. Do not invent a hash. Do not replace the default general and coder pair. Optional picks already noted and not adopted as defaults: AgentHorse Q4_K_M and JEV Qwen3-0.6B.
""",
"09-decision-residency.md": """# title
Decision model should stay available, and MODELS_MAX=1 is still allowed

# body
Kind: needed

Lowram now defaults to `MODELS_MAX=2` so general and coder can both stay loaded. Decision is still not pinned on lowram (`RESIDENT=0`). On moderate and default, decision is pinned and `--models-max` is `MODELS_MAX+1`.

Still open: keep the decision model loaded except while another model is actually working, then bring it back. Explicit `MODELS_MAX=1` is still accepted. One resident model was called unusable for real work.
""",
"10-installer-sha.md": """# title
install.sh and install.ps1 refuse to download until EXPECTED_SHA256 is set

# body
Kind: limitation

Both downloaders fetch the GitHub archive of `main` and refuse while `EXPECTED_SHA256` is empty. The digest cannot be stored only inside the archive it hashes. A published archive has to be hashed, then the scripts updated in a follow-up commit. Tests pass `INSTALL_SHA256`.

`install.sh` and `install.ps1` unpack that archive and exec `start.sh` / `start.bat`. Profile defaults, including `MODELS_MAX=2`, live in `scripts/serve.sh` and `scripts/serve.ps1`, not in the installers.
""",
"11-platform-pass.md": """# title
Platform pass after this sprint

# body
Kind: test

Linux is the baseline. Still to exercise on the machines themselves:

- Windows: `start.bat` / `serve.ps1`, including `HOST` with a LAN address (loopback is added beside it). No PowerShell was available here, so `scripts/lib/bindhost.ps1` was not executed.
- `scripts/start.ps1` still defaults `VARIANT` to `cpu` when unset. Linux `start.sh` defaults to `auto` (cuda-12, else vulkan, else cpu).
- macOS launcher (`start.command` execs `start.sh`). Fetch stays on the cpu build.
- Termux/Android and FreeBSD. Detection exists in places. They are not supported platforms yet.
- A live `llama-server` tool run. This checkout has no `bin/llama-server`, so `tests/test_serve_ready.sh` was not run against a real router.
""",

From this machine:

```sh
curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/brianreborn/code-bootstraps-llama.cpp/main/install.sh | sh
```

That default directory is `~/code-bootstraps-llama.cpp`, which is this checkout. The unpack leaves `.git`, `models/`, and `.secrets/` in place, then runs `start.sh`. Use `INSTALL_PREFIX=/some/other/dir` for a separate copy. `RAISE=0` skips the memlock prompt.

## Assistant

The existing suites passed: agent, remote, recover, install, bindhost, and CPU. Two bugs were real. Both are in `4a7e494` on `main`.

Windows ignored a saved panel. `start.sh` and `serve.sh` read `.cache/panel.env`. `start.ps1` and `serve.ps1` did not, so a setting saved in the form never reached `start.bat`. They read that file now. An environment variable or a parameter such as `-Port` still wins.

`/remote ask` and `/remote take` held the session lock for the whole client run, up to five minutes. They release it before the client starts. `take` also runs in the workspace saved with the brief, not the directory you happen to be in.

The panel's server line was probing port 9931 even after the launcher had moved. It now uses the port in `.cache/serve.ready`.

`install.sh` and `install.ps1` did not need a change. The one-line installer downloads `main`, which now includes this commit. PowerShell is not installed here, so the new `.ps1` path was not executed. [Issue #6](https://github.com/brianreborn/code-bootstraps-llama.cpp/issues/6) stays open: the form still does not cover every launcher setting.

- Edit: /home/green/.grok/sessions/%2Fhome%2Fgreen%2Fcode-bootstraps-llama.cpp/01a10ad3-2a8b-7440-8fb2-f9f5ab74e126/plan.md

## Assistant

Lift is in the plan, and the plan stays open for the rest of the list.

[That post](https://x.com/akshay_pachaar/status/2106361169690956041) is Datalab's [lift](https://github.com/datalab-to/lift): a 9B vision model. You give it a PDF or image and a JSON schema, and it returns one JSON object of that shape, including fields that run across pages. Missing fields come back null. On Datalab's own set it hits 90.2% field accuracy and only 20.9% full-document accuracy, at a median of 9.5 seconds.

In FEELDZNUTTS it is an elective `extract` model, not a third resident next to general and coder. There is no GGUF, so it runs as `/local exec` of `lift_extract`, or as a vLLM the user turns on. The document stays a path. The schema is the transaction block, and a person still confirms the block before anything moves, because a whole document is right only about one time in five.

The code is Apache-2.0. The weights are modified OpenRAIL-M, so they do not go into the one-click installer. Marker, Surya, and Chandra are the same lab's OCR tools and are not committed by this post.

Send the next post when you have it.

- Edit: /home/green/.grok/sessions/%2Fhome%2Fgreen%2Fcode-bootstraps-llama.cpp/01a10ad3-2a8b-7440-8fb2-f9f5ab74e126/plan.md
- Edit: /home/green/.grok/sessions/%2Fhome%2Fgreen%2Fcode-bootstraps-llama.cpp/01a10ad3-2a8b-7440-8fb2-f9f5ab74e126/plan.md

## Assistant

Yes. The front door is `chat`. `start.sh` currently opens `/?model=coder`, and that is the mistake: a session was starting in the specialist. `coder` is a handoff from `chat` or `route`.

An rsync of the workspace would miss the KV. That pool is RAM (`kv-unified` and `cache-ram`). `LLAMA_CACHE` is only a temp directory and does not hold those tokens. Stopping the server first would freeze the KV inside a process that can no longer write it out.

The virtual snapshot does four steps:

1. Tell llama-server to save every live slot, so the KV and prompt cache for `chat`, `coder`, `route`, and `translate` are files.
2. `kill -STOP` the processes that can still change those files.
3. rsync the workspace, `.cache`, the slot dumps, and the session record into one snap area.
4. `kill -CONT`.

The transaction reads that named set. Rollback drops an uncommitted snap. It does not rewind the machine, and it cannot pull back a reply that already left.

## User

we can also consider storing KV on NFS directly. Let's bake NFS3+ support deep into the design as it was made to support distributed systems, and NFS4 especially. Should we support both in all ways? it's another easy transport for certain zones

## Tools

- Read: /home/green/.grok/sessions/%2Fhome%2Fgreen%2Fcode-bootstraps-llama.cpp/01a10ad3-2a8b-7440-8fb2-f9f5ab74e126/plan.md (156-195)
- Read: /home/green/.grok/sessions/%2Fhome%2Fgreen%2Fcode-bootstraps-llama.cpp/01a10ad3-2a8b-7440-8fb2-f9f5ab74e126/plan.md (101-130)

## Assistant

Both versions are in the design. They do not do the same jobs. NFSv3 moves bytes. NFSv4 is the one that can lock and name a commit. A KV file on NFS is a saved slot, written and closed, not a live pool shared by two servers.

## Tools

- Edit: /home/green/.grok/sessions/%2Fhome%2Fgreen%2Fcode-bootstraps-llama.cpp/01a10ad3-2a8b-7440-8fb2-f9f5ab74e126/plan.md
- Edit: /home/green/.grok/sessions/%2Fhome%2Fgreen%2Fcode-bootstraps-llama.cpp/01a10ad3-2a8b-7440-8fb2-f9f5ab74e126/plan.md

## Assistant

Support both, and do not give them the same jobs.

NFSv3 is the byte transport. Old machines already speak it. A finished snapshot, a model file, or a saved KV file can live on a v3 export. It is stateless, so NLM locks are not the transaction lock. The writer closes the file, and only then does another zone open it.

NFSv4, and v4.1 where the server has sessions, is the stateful transport. Lease locks, delegations, and the commit record belong there. `begin` locks the snap name. `commit` publishes the new name and drops the lock. v3 cannot do that.

The live KV pool stays in RAM. NFS holds the slot dump after llama-server has saved it. Two routers do not share one open KV file. The virtual snap area can be the NFS directory, so the rsync writes the tree and those KV files to `nfs://host/snap/<name>/` in one step.

## User

also remember we need to always operate in principle of least privelege and drop privleges rapidly if needed if we start with additional capabilities or admin priv

## Assistant

Privilege stays an exception. A helper may start with a capability for one snapshot or one mount, then drop it before any agent, model, or tool runs. I’ll write that into the plan.

## Assistant

NFSv3 stays. It is not a second client we have to write: the kernel mounts the export, and the snap, the model file, and the saved KV are ordinary files on that path. The writer closes the file before another zone opens it. v3 does not get the commit lock. That lock is NFSv4, or a local file when the server is only v3.

The install is two clicks. The first is `curl | sh` or `irm | iex`. The second is the donation-ware notice: one word in a terminal, one tap on a phone. The install waits for that answer. It does not wait for a payment, and it does not add a third question.

## User

we need to implement user-only privilege-escalated wherever possible

## Assistant

Escalation stays on the user. A root helper is only for the call the operating system refuses to let that user make, and it exits before anything else runs.

## Tools

- Edit: /home/green/.grok/sessions/%2Fhome%2Fgreen%2Fcode-bootstraps-llama.cpp/01a10ad3-2a8b-7440-8fb2-f9f5ab74e126/plan.md

## Assistant

The privileged step keeps the user's uid whenever the operating system can grant that user one right.

The order is: do it as the user with no extra right; if that is not enough, give that same user one capability, a delegated ZFS snapshot permission, a user NFS mount, or a per-user memlock limit; become root only when the kernel refuses those, do the one action, and drop before `exec`. The agent, the router, `/local`, and `/remote` stay on the first step. `kill -STOP` already does, because it applies only to processes this user started.

def walk(x, path=''):
    if isinstance(x, dict):
        for k,v in x.items():
            if k in ('name','tool','title','kind','type','status') and isinstance(v, str) and len(v)<80:
                print(path+k, '=', v[:120])

    sw=$(awk "/^VmSwap/{print \$2}" /proc/$p/status)
    echo "pid=$p role=${role:-router} rss_kb=$rss swap_kb=$sw"
  done
}

- Execute: python3 << 'PY'
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont
rows = [
    ("F", "ederated"),
    ("E", "lectronic"),
    ("E", "lectively-"),
    ("L", "imited"),
    ("D", "istribution-"),
    ("Z", "oned"),
    ("N", "eural Network"),
    ("U", "nified"),
    ("T", "opographical"),
    ("T", "eleportation"),
    ("S", "ystem"),
]
cap_font = ImageFont.truetype("/usr/share/fonts/truetype/lato/Lato-Heavy.ttf", 28)
rest_font = ImageFont.truetype("/usr/share/fonts/truetype/lato/Lato-Regular.ttf", 16)
ink = (31, 58, 95, 255)
rest_c = (90, 104, 120, 255)
paper = (247, 244, 239, 255)
# measure
pad_x, pad_y = 18, 14
gap = 4
row_h = 30
max_w = 0
for cap, rest in rows:
    cb = cap_font.getbbox(cap)
    rb = rest_font.getbbox(rest)
    w = (cb[2]-cb[0]) + 2 + (rb[2]-rb[0])
    max_w = max(max_w, w)
W = pad_x * 2 + max_w + 8
H = pad_y * 2 + row_h * len(rows)
im = Image.new("RGBA", (W, H), paper)
d = ImageDraw.Draw(im)
y = pad_y
for cap, rest in rows:
    cb = cap_font.getbbox(cap)
    rb = rest_font.getbbox(rest)
    cap_h = cb[3] - cb[1]
    rest_h = rb[3] - rb[1]
    # capital sits on the row; the rest of the word drops to the baseline of the capital
    d.text((pad_x - cb[0], y - cb[1]), cap, font=cap_font, fill=ink)
    cap_w = cb[2] - cb[0]
    drop = y + (cap_h - rest_h) + 4
    d.text((pad_x + cap_w + 1 - rb[0], drop - rb[1]), rest, font=rest_font, fill=rest_c)
    y += row_h
out = "/home/green/feeldznutts/logo.png"
im.save(out, "PNG")
print(im.size, out)
PY (Draw a small FEELDZNUTTS acrostic logo)
